Push remains excluded by the saved task boundary. The resulting commit hash is reported in Codex output; this milestone is identified by its subject so the status record stays in the same commit.

The user requested metaprompt-run for slow command execution and approved this plan. Clarification selected tracker CLI reports rather than Codex shell-tool overhead; all finite reports rather than overview alone; two consecutive small rounds rather than stopping after one; and the largest per-case gain rather than total or worst-case runtime.

The compiler owns `prompts/optimize-cli-latency.md`, this plan's compiler evidence, and the matching README navigation link. The fresh executor owns the investigation, focused implementation/regressions, relevant documentation, and this plan's executor evidence. Existing overview profiling/parity tooling is reusable but does not cover every report. Historical cache-hit/fallback measurements must be reproduced before drawing performance conclusions.

## Settled decisions

- Commands: overview, projects, allowance, model-time, history, sessions, health, status. Exclude resume interaction and daemon/import-history throughput.
- Metric: complete subprocess wall time on frozen inputs, one warm-up and five samples per case, median plus spread. Keep the case matrix fixed across rounds.
- Round gain: largest nonnegative per-case median reduction from the previous retained implementation. Stop after two consecutive correct/comparable rounds with gain <1.000s; >=1.000s resets the counter. Failed correctness/noisy rounds do not count. Keep correct smaller improvements; reject confirmed regressions.
- Compare warm, cold, invalidated, supported all-time, and missing/stale-index paths. Preserve full-report JSON parity and freshness.
- The October23 user request authorizes a local commit of verified retained improvements and matching documentation. Keep the unretained R6 candidate outside that commit. Pushing, incompatible installs, live source/data mutation and live service restarts remain excluded. Keep raw identifying evidence private.
- Fresh execution uses the native thread tool once, inherits the current model, and reads the saved prompt without this transcript. Open that same ID in Kitty and inspect its real state.

## Protected initial state and risks

Before any write, `git status --short` showed only these unrelated untracked paths: `docs/postmortem/2026-10-15-overview-task-handoff.md` and `prompts/reduce-overview-startup.md`. Preserve them. No prompt or plan filename collision existed at preparation time.

Timing noise, live data changes, warm caches, and installed/source drift can create false gains; freeze inputs and label provenance. A faster stale report is a correctness failure. Never expose conversation content in public artifacts. If evidence supports no further hypothesis, report the limit rather than fabricate changes. Rollback only task-owned candidate edits; preserve unrelated concurrent work. Compiler documentation can be removed separately if the handoff is abandoned; no runtime activation is implied by launch.

## Exact verification

Compiler: read the saved UTF-8 prompt back, check its report scope, strict 1.000s/two-round rule, sample count, parity/freshness constraints, exclusions, private-artifact rule, and lack of unbound variables. Confirm exact referenced files exist and the complete native handoff is <=1,000 UTF-8 bytes. Inspect the native create/read-thread results; the terminal opener must verify that the target tab is active with the returned ID. These checks validate preparation and handoff, not application performance.

Executor: defining reproducer RED before every fix; focused GREEN plus full frozen JSON parity; then `uv run pytest`, `uv run mypy src`, `uv run ruff check .`, `uv build`, and `./scripts/smoke-test.sh` as relevant. Stopping-rule cases: gains 1.2/0.8/0.6 stop at round 3; 0.4/1.0/0.3/0.2 stop at round 4. Incorrect faster output is rejected; inconclusive noise does not count. Include empty-data, changed-data, supported all-time, and missing/stale/corrupt-cache scenarios.

Direct/manual checks, repository root, no sudo: `time codex-time overview` displays the current overview and elapsed time; `time codex-time history --all-dirs --json` emits history JSON and elapsed time; `time codex-time model-time day --json` emits model-time JSON and elapsed time. These are user-facing smoke checks; the frozen matrix is authoritative for gains.

The native harness is in Default mode, so the already approved decision-complete plan is being executed inline. No new user choice or installation is needed. Repo mapping delegated to search; private snapshot/measurement preparation proceeds separately.

First profiling evidence: baseline cold projects validates 541,367 models; cProfile attributes about 8.8s cumulative to validation and 1.9s to timing snapshot loading. These are diagnostic timings only. Rendering is small. Hypothesis for round 1: reuse the existing guarded `load_overview` period evidence reader for projects/allowance, retaining the same public reports, full authoritative fallback and all-time behavior. Defining tests compare exact JSON with full ledger computation and forbid journal reads on a current index. Scope: cli.py plus index-reader regression tests; no cache format or storage migration.

An exploratory daily-report pruning test was observed RED (1 failed, 3 passed), then withdrawn to keep this first round focused on the dominant read cost; it remains a supported next hypothesis. Private helper review caught 5-second polling quantization before benchmark timing: the initial timeout-wait proposal was superseded by blocking wait with an independent heartbeat so completion is measured without polling quantization. Correct equivalent-state restoration and fixed-clock handling are prerequisites before the measurement matrix can start.

Round 1 RED: `UV_CACHE_DIR=/tmp/tracker-uv-cache uv run pytest -q tests/test_overview_read.py -k other_period_reports` under normal fixture-lock permissions produced 6 expected failures: projects/allowance day/week/month opened legacy journals despite a current index. GREEN after routing both through existing load_overview: `uv run pytest -q tests/test_overview_read.py tests/test_day_cli.py tests/test_command_layout.py tests/test_overview_cache.py` produced 90 passed in 11.69s. These verify fixture parity, all-time behavior, aliases and corrupt/stale index fallback; real frozen parity and performance decision remain pending. Initial restricted fixture setup hit the known canonical home-lock permission boundary; successful elevated checks changed only isolated fixtures/lock files, no live services/data.

Broader profile ranking confirms 541,367 validations in each cold report (projects 8.85s, allowance 9.21s, overview 9.50s cumulative in instrumented runs). Allowance also performs about 25.26 million conflict-range generator iterations. A potential next hypothesis is ordered conflict lookup, but it will not be implemented before the current round is measured. Profile numbers are not performance gain evidence.

Xhigh identified a configured-integer-limit compatibility gate before round4 approval. CPython JSON honors PYTHONINTMAXSTRDIGITS/sys.set_int_max_str_digits; jiter source uses a fixed4300-digit cap. A low configured limit could let the candidate apply an event that stdlib rejects. This is not yet an observed installed-core defect. Add an intentional defining test for limits640/default4300/disabled0/higher6000 before any fix; if confirmed, use existing stdlib decoding for customized limits. No custom numeric parser or regex guard. Required memory query `pydantic core json integer string limit configured` matched generic boundary-contract guidance (reviewed) and unrelated source/network findings, with no verified specific remedy. Actual installed-core reproducer determines applicability.
